# HubSpot local matching-footer preparation

Full65 BaselineA/all13GA/zero whole accepted. Base closed995f6f6d8c417b35e0fd616b2ae6864578894191. User direction: require matching company/address values, explain mismatch, never silently rewrite brand/source or claim live-account validation. Continuous authorized implementation supersedes routine plan/design approval menus; real credentials, legal acceptance, paid commitments and production publication remain gated. Current exact official marketing/CMS2026-09 schemas403, so no provider API revision/request/transport/source publication/deep link invented.

Primary facts 2026-10-03: official HubL variables/email template markup require visible site_settings.company_name/company_street_address_1/company_city/company_state and unsubscribe_link in anchor href, account settings supply actual values; optional address2/zip/country documented. HubLfilters documents escape_html for literal HTML text and escape_url for URLattributes. Settings variables are distinct from unsupported filtered contact/company personalization in emails. Native acceptance of our mapping/filters is unqualified. References https://developers.hubspot.com/docs/cms/reference/hubl/variables ; https://developers.hubspot.com/docs/cms/start-building/building-blocks/templates/email-template-markup ; https://developers.hubspot.com/docs/cms/reference/hubl/filters .


## Exactcontract

Own src/domain/hubspot-footer-contracts.ts and tests/hubspot-footer-contracts.test.ts. No Node/env/server/provider import. Exports:
HUBSPOT_MAPPING_VERSION='hubspot-coded-footer-1'; HUBSPOT_COMPARISON_VERSION='hubspot-footer-comparison-1'; HUBSPOT_SOURCE_LIMIT=2*1024*1024; HUBSPOT_FOOTER_FIELDS readonly ordered seven actual keys below.
HubSpotFooterSettings strict z.object and type HubSpotFooterSettingsData. ALL seven fields required strings: company_name<=500; company_street_address_1/company_street_address_2/company_city/company_state/company_country<=200; company_zip<=50. company_name/street1/city/state nonblank; optionalstreet2/zip/country exactempty allowed, whitespace-only refused. Reject unpairedsurrogates/C0+C1 and authored HubL/foreign delimiters {{/}}/{%/%}/{#/#}/*|/|*/[[/]]/[%/%]. Exact strings preserved withouttrim/casefold; limits UTF16units conservative local, not provider bounds.
composeHubSpotAddress(settings:unknown):string parses strictsettings then deterministic grammar [street1,optionalstreet2,city,state+(zip?' '+zip:''),optionalcountry].join(', '). Composedaddress<=1000 enforced by schema. Grammar is a versioned local comparison convention, not inferred provider formatting. Empty state/otherlayouts refuse until supported, cost boundary correction; never infer components from arbitrarysourceaddress.
checkHubSpotFooterComparison(footers:readonly{identity:string;address:string}[],settings:unknown) returns exactly {matches:true,settings_origin:'locally_declared',account_settings_verified:false} or throws fresh fixed Error. Inputfooters are extracted identity/address pairs only, nonempty and allwellformednonblank/max500/1000; malformed/empty footer =>EXPORT_FOOTER_REQUIRED. Settings invalid/missing/unknown =>HUBSPOT_SETTINGS_INVALID. Any identity/address mismatch vs exact company_name/composedaddress =>HUBSPOT_FOOTER_MISMATCH. Never mutate source/settings; no private values or Zod details in errors/results. Test comparison with actual EmailSpec frozen-fixture extraction.
HUBSPOT_FOOTER_MESSAGES fixed browsercopy for two newcodes: invalid/missing asks supply accountfooter values forcomparison/accountaccessunverified; mismatch explains company/address differs, savedfooter unchanged, usematching values. No rawvalues/fields echoed.
HubSpotReview strict Zod schema+type same name. Fields destination:'hubspot',mapping_version literal,comparison_version literal,revision_id strictz.uuid,source_artifact_hash/destination_hash/html_sha256/text_sha256/settings_digest lowercase64hex,settings_origin:'locally_declared', remote_export_enabled/account_settings_verified/native_conformance_verified/management_link_verified allliteralfalse; transformations array1..10 strings nonblank<=500; exactordered blockers tuple CONNECTION_AUTH_MODE_UNAPPROVED,HUBSPOT_ACCOUNT_SETTINGS_UNVERIFIED,ACCOUNT_ENTITLEMENT_UNVERIFIED,REAL_CLIENT_PREFLIGHT_UNAVAILABLE,DESTINATION_CONFORMANCE_UNVERIFIED,DURABLE_REMOTE_EXPORT_UNAVAILABLE,MANAGEMENT_LINK_UNVERIFIED. No HTML/text/rawsettings/subject/APIrevision/managementURL/secretfields permitted.
HubSpotReviewInput strict{settings:HubSpotFooterSettings}. HubSpotArtifactInput strict{settings,format:'html'|'txt',expected_destination_hash:lowercase64hex}. No readiness/client/provider evidence accepted from body. Local bodymax16KiB laterserverroute.


Important: schema compoundaddress refinement must call private raw compose helper, not recursively call public compose which parses the same schema. No source/brand/inputmutation. Bundleverificationoutput must go outside repo (e.g/tmp/lettercape-hubspot-footer-task1-browser.mjs), avoiding generatedthirdparty ESLintwarnings. Retain executable permissive scaffold as text outside activeproduction/testpaths, allRED/GREEN/failure logs. Do not run fullconfiguredsuite/DB/browser, no network/provider/privateenv/PRD. Own exact2files, rootownsdocs/plan/ledger and laterqualification. Read local AGENTS and relevant installedNextguide before code; onlybrowserdomain code noNextAPI needed. The test was corrected; production code was not weakened.

An invocation mistake occurred during the first RED attempt: `npm test -- --test-name-pattern='strict settings|settings reject|invalid composed|comparison accepts|comparison rejects|comparison reads|comparison errors|browser copy|review and request' tests/hubspot-footer-contracts.test.ts`. Because the package script expands `tests/*.test.ts`, this launched the full suite. The captured output was truncated by the command tool (2,970 output lines / 51,535-token estimate). The visible output showed unrelated database cases receiving `AggregateError [EPERM]` for `connect ::1:5432` and `connect 127.0.0.1:5432`, database fixtures reporting `Owned local database required`, and loopback cases receiving `listen EPERM` for `127.0.0.1`. Exact suite counts and the complete raw stdout/stderr were not retained, so there is no honest complete-log hash to provide. The previous report preserved only this outcome summary; it did not preserve a complete historical RED/intermediate/GREEN raw log. The command is not being rerun and its output is not being reconstructed. This historical scope/evidence failure remains unresolved and must not be described as satisfied. No provider operation, external network call, private environment/PRD access, deployment, or spend occurred; the broad suite did attempt local database and socket operations before the sandbox denied them.

## Contract self-review

- Exactly seven ordered settings keys are required by a strict object. Values are kept byte-for-byte as JavaScript strings; no trim/case folding or inferred address parsing occurs. Required identity/street/city/state reject blank text. The three optional fields accept only exact empty or nonblank text.
- String limits use JavaScript UTF-16 code units. C0/C1 controls, malformed surrogate sequences, HubL delimiters, Mailchimp-style `*|…|*`, and the specified foreign template delimiters are rejected. A supplementary Unicode pair remains accepted.
- Address composition follows the brief’s local comma-and-space convention and strict settings refinement enforces its 1,000-unit total limit. This is a local comparison convention, not a claim about HubSpot account rendering.
- Comparison validates every supplied footer pair, rejects missing/malformed pairs with `EXPORT_FOOTER_REQUIRED`, rejects invalid settings with `HUBSPOT_SETTINGS_INVALID`, and rejects any identity/address mismatch with `HUBSPOT_FOOTER_MISMATCH`. Errors are newly created and fixed; no input values or schema details enter error messages or receipt.
- The actual `EmailSpec` comes from `blankSpec`, receives a fixture address, is deep-frozen after cloning, and is extracted through its legal-footer section. Tests confirm both the frozen extracted object and the editable source remain unchanged.
- Review/request schemas are strict; review requires the exact ordered blockers, false verification/export flags, locally declared settings origin, version/hash/UUID constraints, and bounded nonblank transformations. Request bodies accept only their listed settings/format/hash fields. No provider credentials, private HTML/text, readiness evidence, API revision, or management link is accepted.
- The module imports only Zod and has no Node, server, provider, environment, or network dependency; the browser bundle succeeded.

## Limitations and remaining work

The schemas express a local comparison and review contract. They do not verify live account settings, provider formatting, account entitlement, native HubL rendering, or a management URL. The brief explicitly leaves those claims unresolved. The repository already contained untracked `tooling/openapi/node_modules`; it was not read or changed. Task 1’s code/test changes are intended to be committed as exactly those two paths; this report remains an uncommitted handoff artifact for the parent task.

## Follow-up review round 1

Finding I1 was reproduced and fixed. A sparse array with a matching footer in a later slot previously passed because `.some()` skips holes. `checkHubSpotFooterComparison` now checks every numeric index for an own property, validates each footer pair, stores parsed pairs in a dense local array, and compares every parsed entry. This rejects empty and mixed-valid-plus-hole arrays with fixed `EXPORT_FOOTER_REQUIRED`; no source or input is mutated.

The new focused regression was written before the fix. Command: `node --import tsx --test tests/hubspot-footer-contracts.test.ts`. The RED result was 9 pass / 1 fail; the new test failed with `AssertionError: Missing expected exception.` and command status 1. Complete output is retained at `/tmp/lettercape-hubspot-footer-task1-sparse-red.log` (40 lines, 2,112 bytes; SHA-256 `0d3a0442df871beec8d8c01d729294595fd86510394dd400ada1320e7a5b6cf3`).

The review’s observed-output probe for `new Array(1)` returned `{"matches":true,"settings_origin":"locally_declared","account_settings_verified":false}` with exit 0; the expected result was `EXPORT_FOOTER_REQUIRED`. This is preserved as the review’s observed output, not as a newly run or reconstructed historical log.

After the fix, exact focused command `node --import tsx --test tests/hubspot-footer-contracts.test.ts` passed 10/10, exit 0. Complete log: `/tmp/lettercape-hubspot-footer-task1-sparse-green.log` (20 lines, 1,172 bytes; SHA-256 `e1ad870ceff92e270a0a9c758f871ba2384a1c26522835a327208af65373255d`).

# Task 2 report: frozen visible-footer compiler

Date: 2026-10-03  
Checkout: `/tmp/lettercape-hubspot-footer-native`  
Base: `20ca82b83b4a92a37800c7eab63f2d7c3f34fe3f`  
Commit: `fa99bd3900a13c4fe916d440eeb09c59f686ca5c`

## Outcome and exact scope

Implemented `compileHubSpotArtifact(revision, settings)` and `hubspotReview(artifact)` in `src/domain/hubspot-export.ts`, with 57 behavior tests in `tests/hubspot-export.test.ts`. The local commit changes exactly those two paths. The root-owned plan modification and pre-existing untracked `tooling/openapi/node_modules` were neither staged nor changed. This report remains an ignored handoff artifact, outside the exact two-file commit.

The compiler first calls the unchanged `validateFrozenExportSource`. It captures a strict validated settings snapshot once, compares every flattened source legal-footer pair with the exact local address convention, refuses authored HubL/foreign syntax after removing only canonical unsubscribe slots for inspection, and maps verified footer spans without reserializing the document or globally replacing company/address values. The artifact extends the strict review shape with only `html` and `text`.

All complete test/check logs, executable RED scaffold, original RED test snapshot, exact implementation patch, browser contract bundle, and deterministic synthetic sample files are retained outside the checkout under `/tmp/lettercape-hubspot-footer-task2-evidence/`. `command-ledger.md` records exact commands, exits, counts, and the initial wrapper/fixture corrections. `sha256-manifest.json` records byte counts and SHA-256 for every retained evidence file (except itself).

## TDD evidence and all encountered failures

1. Wrote the real frozen-fixture behavior tests and an executable compiler scaffold exporting the intended API but throwing `HUBSPOT_COMPILER_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`. The scaffold and tests are saved as `red-scaffold.ts` and `red-tests.ts`. No missing-module/import failure was used as RED evidence.
2. Original RED command: `node --import tsx --test tests/hubspot-export.test.ts`, exit 1, 0 pass / 56 fail, complete `red.log`. Of these, 55 failures were the deliberately absent compiler behavior (unexpected scaffold exception or wrong expected error). The mapped-HTML boundary test additionally exposed an invalid large fixture: Unicode padding exceeded the shared structured-spec 1 MiB and per-text 10,000-unit limits. The outer zsh wrapper also attempted to assign to its readonly `status` variable after the test had completed; it returned 1 with `read-only variable: status`. The complete test log was already saved. Later wrappers use `check_exit`.
3. Corrected the boundary fixture to use HTML ampersand expansion from a valid smaller spec. Reran the same RED command before implementing production behavior: exit 1, 0 pass / 56 fail, complete `red-corrected.log`. All failures were the deliberately missing scaffold behavior; no Zod/import/reference/fixture exception remained. Full failed-test names and assertions are preserved in that log.
4. Implemented mapping and metadata. First GREEN command: same exact scoped command, exit 1, 55 pass / 1 fail, complete `green-first.log`. The one failure was `visible native HTML and anchored local plaintext preserve every byte outside verified footer spans`: its fixture assertion assumed the shared structured renderer emitted `<!--[if mso]>` and a `<style>` block. Inspection showed it emits React comments and inline style attributes instead. Corrected those assertions to the actual existing comments/styles; did not inject invalid frozen bytes or change source integrity.
5. First full typecheck: `node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit`, exit 2, `typecheck-first.log`. It reported 10 compiler TypeScript narrowing diagnostics (possibly undefined node/location/href, possibly null parent, and un-narrowed paragraph childNodes), plus one test fixture diagnostic where `unsubscribe_slot` inferred boolean instead of literal true. Declared the always-throwing integrity helper as a function returning `never` so TypeScript narrows those guarded paths, and marked the footer fixture boolean `as const`. These were type fixes without loosening runtime guards.
6. Self-review identified repeated reads of the raw `unknown` settings object. Added `settings are captured once so comparison and digest cannot observe different declared values` before fixing it. Command: `node --import tsx --test --test-name-pattern 'settings are captured once' tests/hubspot-export.test.ts`, exit 1, 0 pass / 1 fail, complete `red-settings-snapshot.log`; failure was observed read count `2 !== 1`. The compiler now validates/captures the supplied settings once, compares the plain parsed snapshot, and uses that same snapshot for mapping/digests. Invalid settings retain fixed `HUBSPOT_SETTINGS_INVALID` instead of a second parse exposing schema details.
7. Final scoped GREEN: `node --import tsx --test tests/hubspot-export.test.ts`, exit 0, 57 tests / 57 pass / 0 fail / 0 skipped, complete `green-scoped.log`.

## Self-review against the binding spec

- **Source and error precedence:** Existing frozen validation is the first executable preparation step. HTML/plaintext limits and types, revision ID/hash/manifest/spec integrity, static blocking findings, raw/custom sources, missing footers, private manifests/asset references/private delivery bindings all retain their existing fixed errors before settings processing. Tests corrupt both outputs, footer HTML structure/href attributes, manifest metadata/nested fields/array ordering, spec, ID and hash; JSONB object key ordering remains accepted.
- **Strict settings and comparison:** Strict settings are validated once from supplied input and only the parsed local copy is used. No normalization or arbitrary address inference occurs. Tests cover matching and differing identity/address, missing/unknown/unsafe/malformed/control values, exact whitespace, optional empties, and a later/nested mismatching footer. All settings/comparison rejection assertions require exact fixed errors. Source and supplied settings are deep-frozen in success and mismatch tests and remain unchanged.
- **Unsupported syntax:** HTML and plaintext are independently inspected after only `{{UNSUBSCRIBE_URL}}` removal. HubL expressions/tags/comments, dangling delimiters, Mailchimp delimiters, square-bracket foreign delimiters, authored canonical slots in other content, and canonical-looking CTA hrefs refuse with `EXPORT_TOKEN_UNSUPPORTED`. Each format must contain exactly `footerCount` canonical slots before mapping.
- **HTML proof and replacement:** parse5 uses `sourceCodeLocationInfo: true`. Document-order canonical href anchors must equal flattened footer count. Every immediate parent is an HTML `div` with exactly paragraph + anchor children. Paragraph children are exactly identity text + empty `br` + address text; DOM-decoded text must equal the corresponding frozen pair. The unsubscribe label is also checked. Existing class/data-unsubscribe attributes refuse. Identity/address raw slices are independently decoded as a single text node and checked against exact source values. The canonical raw href span must equal `href="{{UNSUBSCRIBE_URL}}"`. Every replacement is bounds/length/ordered-nonoverlap/raw-span checked before backward slice collection/reverse/join. No DOM serialization and no global company/address rewrite occur. Only the href attribute span gains native unsubscribe URL escaping and the exact documented `class="hubspot-mergetag" data-unsubscribe="true"` attributes; the original surrounding attributes and style bytes remain.
- **Visible native footer:** HTML name/address use `site_settings.*|escape_html`; unsubscribe href uses `unsubscribe_link|escape_url`. Optional street2/ZIP/country expressions are included only when the exact parsed declared field is nonempty. Separators match the versioned local comparison grammar, including the one literal space between state and optional ZIP. Supplementary Unicode, entity decoding, literal whitespace, nested/repeated footers and nonfooter repeated company/address values are exercised.
- **Anchored plaintext:** Canonical slots are enumerated in order. Each exact contiguous preceding `identity + '\n' + address + '\nUnsubscribe: '` prefix is proved and the full footer span is replaced with bare native expressions. Spans cannot overlap or consume earlier footer regions. Other plaintext bytes remain unchanged. The shared compiler still supplies the frozen projection; its formatter was not copied.
- **Hashing and privacy:** Settings digest hashes UTF-8 `JSON.stringify([comparison_version, ...the seven exact ordered values])`. Destination hash hashes `JSON.stringify([mapping_version, comparison_version, source_hash, settings_digest, html, text])`. HTML/text SHA-256 bind exact UTF-8 output. Independent ordered literal values are used in tests; reversed input object keys do not change the result. `hubspotReview` removes only HTML/text and validates the remaining strict metadata, refusing unknown properties and true evidence flags. Transformations are fixed content-free strings. Review contains no raw settings, content, subject, API revision, credentials or management URL. Readiness/export/account/native/link flags remain false with the exact ordered seven blockers.
- **Budgets and I/O:** Each mapped format is explicitly limited to <=2 MiB UTF-8 as a conservative local budget. The mapped HTML test accepts exactly 2,097,152 bytes and refuses 2,097,153 while frozen source still fits. Local preparation imports no server/provider/credential resolver and does not use fetch; a real compile test makes global fetch throw, while source/settings remain frozen and repeated results remain deterministic. Browser consumers continue to use the separate Node-free contract module; its browser bundle succeeds.

## Limits and concerns

There is no remaining scoped implementation/check blocker. Native/raw/client acceptance remains unqualified: no HubSpot account settings access, account entitlement, HubL provider rendering, native plaintext handoff, management URL/link conformance, real-client preflight, durable remote export or remote auth mode was verified. Plaintext is explicitly a local companion only. Provider rendering/address conventions must not be inferred from this local deterministic grammar or the emitted variable names.

The current accepted structured shared compiler emits React comments and inline style attributes, but provides no valid conditional-comment/VML or `<style>` block fixture. Exact complete HTML-string equality outside mapped footer bytes is tested against its actual comments/styles; conditional/VML preservation is implied by the span algorithm, not demonstrated with an accepted frozen fixture. Injecting such bytes would correctly fail existing frozen integrity, and raw/custom sources remain unsupported. Tests do not bypass that validation to manufacture native/raw acceptance evidence.

A mapped plaintext 2 MiB boundary is unreachable through the current shared 1 MiB structured-spec budget and maximum 200 sections. The explicit plaintext budget guard is implemented, and source plaintext size/type limits plus exact plaintext bytes are tested; an accepted frozen artifact reaching the mapped-text boundary was not fabricated. Additional structural refusal branches are defense-in-depth checks after frozen integrity; malformed output fixture tests correctly refuse at the earlier frozen validation rather than claiming direct branch coverage.

The root owns mounted route/UI/request-body/review flows and real journey verification after Task 2. No subagents were spawned.

## Self-review against brief

- Seven values are always present and raw strings; optional empties are retained. Snapshot validates strict contracts and freezes a separate parsed object. No source/footer/brand/account state mutation or persistence of settings occurs.
- Workspace/actor/email mismatch masks old rendered settings immediately; layout clears settings ref and increments/aborts the existing generation; existing load path clears settings state and receipt. Destination switching keeps explicit same-context settings but clears receipt and increments/aborts. A→B→A invalidates by generation even when final values/destination match.
- Before freeze, invalid settings produce fixed safe copy and no checkpoint. Initial freeze captures exact settings/generation; optional guards prevent dispatch after changed settings/scope during flush. If freeze already completed during a settings/destination interruption, its acknowledged immutable revision remains available while the older review interval returns without posting/adopting. Scope-invalid completion cannot install its freeze receipt.
- After freeze, network, settings digest await, and immediately before adoption, exact generation/destination/context/lifecycle/source checks are required. Abort suppresses errors. Existing synchronous `busyRef` admission remains in `act`.
- Strict public HubSpot schema rejects raw settings/extra content and false-to-true readiness substitutions. Revision ID/source hash must match the freeze; settings digest uses browser SHA-256 over comparison version followed by seven ordered exact strings.
- Artifact POST uses reviewed settings/format/expected destination hash only in the JSON body; scope headers and abort signal are present. Current generation and all seven settings must still match before dispatch. Existing source/lifecycle/generation checks follow response, chunks, SHA, and precede Blob adoption. Existing five required receipt headers retained; server supplies no settings/comparison headers, so none invented. Actual stream size is bounded at 2 MiB and exact bytes SHA-checked. Failed HubSpot verification clears receipt and shows private-safe copy.
- Prior four destination mapping schemas and GET endpoint/query shapes preserved. New helper imports only browser-safe contracts and existing api; bundle independently excludes source server modules.

## Guards, privacy and instrumentation boundary

Read AGENTS, installed Next CLI guide, verification-before-completion, test-driven-development and writing-good-tests instructions before writing. Existing product already implemented; this is a real executable acceptance harness, not production feature work. Faulted/held responses test the actual mounted application's refusal/adoption behavior rather than a fake UI/model. The first execution failures were real harness issues listed below; no artificial constant failure or product mutation was claimed as TDD proof.

Only existing `sourceDatabase` normal trusted configuration loading was used. No private env/PRD file was read, copied or hashed. Fixture sessions/API keys/signing secrets are newly synthetic; configuration values and credentials are never printed. Helper requires LOCAL_DEVELOPMENT and loopback PostgreSQL. Harness rechecks loopback runtime/migration hosts and exact `creation_fixture_[a-f0-9]{32}` DB name; app uses only that owned DB and port 3004. Occupied-port preflight refuses to touch an existing listener.

Browser-context HTTP requests outside the owned origin are routed, counted and aborted. Service-worker registration is denied by a harness init script; only the navigator.serviceWorker access SecurityError in opaque sandbox frames is caught, other guard errors are rethrown. The pageerror collector remains active and filters nothing; final collector records full available stacks. Browser WebSockets, process-wide/network-level egress and every OS socket are not claimed instrumented. No provider/OAuth/credential resolver, spend, campaign, recipient/media worker or delivery path was invoked. Main 3003/Desktop 3002/other 3000 processes were never targeted.

**Interruption scope:** original response-hold tests fully fetch the actual response before delivering headers/body to the browser. The extension additionally reaches two actual editor await boundaries. For later chunk reading, a browser fetch wrapper fetches the real owned artifact, clones its actual bytes, preserves real response headers/status, and schedules those same bytes through a two-pull ReadableStream with highWaterMark zero. It enqueues the first half and holds the second pull until settings ABA completes. With no prefetch buffering, the second pull is driven by the real editor reader.read. For SHA, the wrapper observes actual bytes from a clone without replacing the real Response; a crypto.subtle.digest wrapper matches the complete exact downloaded byte sequence, invokes the original native digest, and holds the caller-facing promise until settings ABA completes. Native crypto work can finish during the hold; this proves the editor digest-await result fence, not hardware computation still running. Other digest calls proceed immediately. Window fetch and crypto.subtle.digest are restored exactly in finally and the transient test property is deleted. Original route-held tests additionally cover destination ABA and actual email/workspace/actor changes; late-chunk/SHA extension specifically covers settings ABA. No product class or generation model was added; the final pageerror collector stays active without exception filtering. The injected scheduling code is static JavaScript to avoid tsx function serialization helpers.

Prior commits, run reports and old artifact manifests are not rewritten.

## Finding and fix

The reviewed helper `hold.release()` remains a **producer** route-callback barrier; it is not relabeled as browser consumer completion. The fix adds a persistent actual-browser download observer whose forbidden-file predicate matches the product's `hubspot-prepared-` filename prefix. It retains filename, current page URL, synthetic current actor/workspace context and test phase for every observed file. Forbidden events remain recorded instead of being absorbed into another baseline.

A single immutable download count baseline spans **all** email/workspace/actor scope-transition cases. Assertions run before each hold, immediately after producer release, after the actual fresh editor acknowledgment/review/action readiness completes, and at the complete scope-group boundary before any legitimate later downloads. The guard then remains installed through the four legitimate other-provider download journeys, final Viewer checks and **awaited browser closure**. Those later destinations have distinct allowed filename prefixes; any late HubSpot file is still forbidden. The final scope manifest is written and guard checked after browser/app cleanup. This is the equivalent persistent forbidden-download qualification requested by I1; no inference that route delivery settles the editor is required.

## Meaningful delayed-adoption sensitivity

The **same guard implementation** is exercised first with a deliberately deferred **real browser file event**. The mounted page fetches actual owned HTTP HubSpot artifact bytes with the actual review hash, validates the actual content-SHA header, and prepares a Blob from those real bytes. Production and receipt/body reading have returned before an explicit scheduling gate permits a delayed anchor download. The old immediate count assertion passes at **7→7**. The deferred real event then increments the count to **8**, uses filename `hubspot-prepared-delayed-sensitivity.html`, and the persistent guard's actual check must throw `Forbidden delayed HubSpot download`. The resulting downloaded file completes successfully and independently hashes to the actual receipt SHA:

`51ed60c40b7e86d816460e10be49de1c50b9f0416a3c0d3287768b4812e51413`.

This proves sensitivity to the missed event timing with real browser output; it does not simulate editor generation state, modify app code or claim the product itself adopted a stale file. The intentional sensitivity guard is removed after its event/file completes. Its temporary scheduling property is deleted by the callback; Blob URL is revoked. The subsequent genuine transition group begins at constant baseline **8**, so this expected fault-injection event is neither hidden nor counted as a product failure.

## task5-fix1-review complete review

### Finding Verdicts

- **I1 — Scope-transition no-file assertions can precede browser adoption — ADDRESSED.** The fix adds a persistent browser download observer that records filename, page URL, phase and synthetic actor/workspace, and permanently records any `hubspot-prepared-` event (`scripts/smoke-hubspot-export.ts:37`–`:47`). One immutable baseline now spans the full email/workspace/actor group (`:574`–`:580`), with checks before each hold, after producer release, after actual fresh editor review/readiness recovery and at group end (`:588`, `:600`–`:608`). It no longer absorbs late files into a newly captured per-transition baseline. The observer remains installed during subsequent legitimate four-provider downloads and final Viewer checks, and its final check executes after awaited browser close and app/log cleanup (`:661`, `:668`–`:674`). A HubSpot event arriving after an early producer assertion therefore remains forbidden and cannot be hidden by later allowed destination files.
- **I1 sensitivity and retained evidence — VERIFIED.** The same observer is exercised with a deliberately deferred real browser download of actual owned HTTP artifact bytes (`scripts/smoke-hubspot-export.ts:534`–`:569`). The old immediate assertion passes at 7→7; the subsequent actual file increments 7→8, the observer records its HubSpot filename/context, and `assert.throws` proves rejection before the sensitivity listener is removed (`:561`–`:569`). ## Strengths

The handler enters existing current edit/export authority and tenant transaction boundaries before reading settings. ## Issues

### Critical — must fix

None found.

### Important — should fix

None found in the reviewed final branch. Historical process/evidence qualifications are retained below and are not reclassified as newly introduced product defects.

### Minor — nice to have

**M1 — The delayed-download sensitivity probe can falsely fail on a slow controller.**

- **Location:** `scripts/smoke-hubspot-export.ts:550`–`:561` (the 50 ms scheduled anchor click and immediate counter assertion).
- **Problem:** The probe releases a timer inside the browser, awaits the Playwright evaluation round trip, then asserts that no intentional download event has arrived. More than 50 ms of browser/controller scheduling delay can allow that event to arrive before the assertion. This is a real race in the harness, even when the persistent forbidden-file observer works correctly.
- **Impact/severity:** A valid qualification can fail spuriously, consuming investigation/rerun time. It cannot falsely accept stale product output: an early sensitivity event fails the assertion, and the genuine transition observer retains all forbidden events. Both the retained fix run and current root qualification pass the probe. This remains **nonblocking Minor**, independently confirming the deferred Task 5 M1; it is not a new product safety finding and does not reopen the fixed scope-guard Important.
- **Correction:** Keep the intentional anchor click behind an explicit browser gate while asserting the baseline, then release the click and await the real download/event rejection. No wall-clock delay is needed to establish “after the old assertion.” If changed, run the covering owned smoke and scoped checks required for that harness change; do not rerun unrelated qualification merely for this review.

**Final open counts: Critical 0 / Important 0 / Minor 1.**

## Finding disposition

### M1 — The delayed-download sensitivity probe can falsely fail on a slow controller

**ADDRESSED.**

The previous 50 ms scheduling delay is removed at `scripts/smoke-hubspot-export.ts:550`–`:554`. Preparing the real server Blob installs a held `release` callback without clicking the anchor. Release now clicks synchronously. The remaining 1,000 ms timer at `:553` only revokes the object URL after the click; it does not gate event ordering.

At `:558`–`:562`, the controller installs the pending download-event promise, asserts `downloads === baseline` while the Blob remains held, and then invokes the callback. Controller/browser scheduling latency can no longer cause this intentional download to arrive before that baseline assertion. The actual file completion, failure check, downloaded-byte SHA comparison, persistent guard rejection, and single-forbidden-event assertion remain at `:563`–`:567`. The evidence record at `:568` explicitly names the pre-release count barrier.

This implements the specified correction without weakening the real-event sensitivity proof. The fix does not alter the immutable ordinary-transition baseline or observer lifetime shown in the package context. No new correctness, maintainability, or scope issue was identified in the changed lines.

No tests or qualification commands were rerun for this review. I read these existing correction-run outputs and independently matched their SHA-256 values to the correction report:

## Evidence limits and boundaries

The reviewed package and retained artifacts support this scoped approval. Empty lint/type output alone does not independently encode the process exit status; exit-zero statuses and diff-check outcomes are taken from the supplied correction report. Source hash, exact commit/file ownership, artifact preservation, and the byte-identical materialization of the separate scratch report copy were verified by the controller and were not repeated here. The preflight error text reports an occupied port, but does not itself prove the underlying sandbox bind-error cause.

No unchanged-code check was necessary. I did not read private files, invoke providers, inspect or mutate databases, start an app/browser, use the network, crawl git, reread changed source, or modify the checkout. The only created file is this requested review report. This verdict does not expand local acceptance into provider/native-account or GA qualification, and does not close previously retained historical qualification gaps.

# Task 1 report: strict comparison and browser contracts

## TDD and verification evidence

This was behavioral failure, not a missing import. Scaffold text and its hash are retained above.

The two failures exposed test assertions that needed correction: the source fixture was intentionally populated with `Suite 7`, and the copy said “has not been verified” while the test matched a narrower phrase. The test was corrected; production code was not weakened.

An invocation mistake occurred during the first RED attempt: `npm test -- --test-name-pattern='strict settings|settings reject|invalid composed|comparison accepts|comparison rejects|comparison reads|comparison errors|browser copy|review and request' tests/hubspot-footer-contracts.test.ts`. Because the package script expands `tests/*.test.ts`, this launched the full suite. The captured output was truncated by the command tool (2,970 output lines / 51,535-token estimate). The visible output showed unrelated database cases receiving `AggregateError [EPERM]` for `connect ::1:5432` and `connect 127.0.0.1:5432`, database fixtures reporting `Owned local database required`, and loopback cases receiving `listen EPERM` for `127.0.0.1`. Exact suite counts and the complete raw stdout/stderr were not retained, so there is no honest complete-log hash to provide. The previous report preserved only this outcome summary; it did not preserve a complete historical RED/intermediate/GREEN raw log. The command is not being rerun and its output is not being reconstructed. This historical scope/evidence failure remains unresolved and must not be described as satisfied. No provider operation, external network call, private environment/PRD access, deployment, or spend occurred; the broad suite did attempt local database and socket operations before the sandbox denied them.

## Contract self-review

- Exactly seven ordered settings keys are required by a strict object. Values are kept byte-for-byte as JavaScript strings; no trim/case folding or inferred address parsing occurs. Required identity/street/city/state reject blank text. The three optional fields accept only exact empty or nonblank text.
- String limits use JavaScript UTF-16 code units. C0/C1 controls, malformed surrogate sequences, HubL delimiters, Mailchimp-style `*|…|*`, and the specified foreign template delimiters are rejected. A supplementary Unicode pair remains accepted.
- Address composition follows the brief’s local comma-and-space convention and strict settings refinement enforces its 1,000-unit total limit. This is a local comparison convention, not a claim about HubSpot account rendering.
- Comparison validates every supplied footer pair, rejects missing/malformed pairs with `EXPORT_FOOTER_REQUIRED`, rejects invalid settings with `HUBSPOT_SETTINGS_INVALID`, and rejects any identity/address mismatch with `HUBSPOT_FOOTER_MISMATCH`. Errors are newly created and fixed; no input values or schema details enter error messages or receipt.
- The actual `EmailSpec` comes from `blankSpec`, receives a fixture address, is deep-frozen after cloning, and is extracted through its legal-footer section. Tests confirm both the frozen extracted object and the editable source remain unchanged.
- Review/request schemas are strict; review requires the exact ordered blockers, false verification/export flags, locally declared settings origin, version/hash/UUID constraints, and bounded nonblank transformations. Request bodies accept only their listed settings/format/hash fields. No provider credentials, private HTML/text, readiness evidence, API revision, or management link is accepted.
- The module imports only Zod and has no Node, server, provider, environment, or network dependency; the browser bundle succeeded.

## Limitations and remaining work

The schemas express a local comparison and review contract. They do not verify live account settings, provider formatting, account entitlement, native HubL rendering, or a management URL. The brief explicitly leaves those claims unresolved. The repository already contained untracked `tooling/openapi/node_modules`; it was not read or changed. Task 1’s code/test changes are intended to be committed as exactly those two paths; this report remains an uncommitted handoff artifact for the parent task.

## Follow-up review round 1

Finding I1 was reproduced and fixed. A sparse array with a matching footer in a later slot previously passed because `.some()` skips holes. `checkHubSpotFooterComparison` now checks every numeric index for an own property, validates each footer pair, stores parsed pairs in a dense local array, and compares every parsed entry. This rejects empty and mixed-valid-plus-hole arrays with fixed `EXPORT_FOOTER_REQUIRED`; no source or input is mutated.

The new focused regression was written before the fix. Command: `node --import tsx --test tests/hubspot-footer-contracts.test.ts`. The RED result was 9 pass / 1 fail; the new test failed with `AssertionError: Missing expected exception.` and command status 1. ## Guards, privacy and instrumentation boundary

Read AGENTS, installed Next CLI guide, verification-before-completion, test-driven-development and writing-good-tests instructions before writing. Existing product already implemented; this is a real executable acceptance harness, not production feature work. Faulted/held responses test the actual mounted application's refusal/adoption behavior rather than a fake UI/model. The first execution failures were real harness issues listed below; no artificial constant failure or product mutation was claimed as TDD proof.

Only existing `sourceDatabase` normal trusted configuration loading was used. No private env/PRD file was read, copied or hashed. Fixture sessions/API keys/signing secrets are newly synthetic; configuration values and credentials are never printed. Helper requires LOCAL_DEVELOPMENT and loopback PostgreSQL. Harness rechecks loopback runtime/migration hosts and exact `creation_fixture_[a-f0-9]{32}` DB name; app uses only that owned DB and port 3004. Occupied-port preflight refuses to touch an existing listener.

Browser-context HTTP requests outside the owned origin are routed, counted and aborted. Service-worker registration is denied by a harness init script; only the navigator.serviceWorker access SecurityError in opaque sandbox frames is caught, other guard errors are rethrown. The pageerror collector remains active and filters nothing; final collector records full available stacks. Browser WebSockets, process-wide/network-level egress and every OS socket are not claimed instrumented. No provider/OAuth/credential resolver, spend, campaign, recipient/media worker or delivery path was invoked. Main 3003/Desktop 3002/other 3000 processes were never targeted.

**Interruption scope:** actual server response is fully fetched by the route interceptor, then held before browser delivery of headers/body. Settings/destination/navigation/workspace/actor changes happen at that held-response boundary. SDK similarly holds the actual Response before giving it to the real client. This task did **not** delay an individual browser `reader.read()` chunk or `crypto.subtle.digest()` await and makes no claim of late chunk/SHA race qualification. That is a remaining root/Task 4 coverage consideration, not an observed defect.

## Guards, privacy and instrumentation boundary

Read AGENTS, installed Next CLI guide, verification-before-completion, test-driven-development and writing-good-tests instructions before writing. Existing product already implemented; this is a real executable acceptance harness, not production feature work. Faulted/held responses test the actual mounted application's refusal/adoption behavior rather than a fake UI/model. The first execution failures were real harness issues listed below; no artificial constant failure or product mutation was claimed as TDD proof.

Only existing `sourceDatabase` normal trusted configuration loading was used. No private env/PRD file was read, copied or hashed. Fixture sessions/API keys/signing secrets are newly synthetic; configuration values and credentials are never printed. Helper requires LOCAL_DEVELOPMENT and loopback PostgreSQL. Harness rechecks loopback runtime/migration hosts and exact `creation_fixture_[a-f0-9]{32}` DB name; app uses only that owned DB and port 3004. Occupied-port preflight refuses to touch an existing listener.

Browser-context HTTP requests outside the owned origin are routed, counted and aborted. Service-worker registration is denied by a harness init script; only the navigator.serviceWorker access SecurityError in opaque sandbox frames is caught, other guard errors are rethrown. The pageerror collector remains active and filters nothing; final collector records full available stacks. Browser WebSockets, process-wide/network-level egress and every OS socket are not claimed instrumented. No provider/OAuth/credential resolver, spend, campaign, recipient/media worker or delivery path was invoked. Main 3003/Desktop 3002/other 3000 processes were never targeted.

**Interruption scope:** original response-hold tests fully fetch the actual response before delivering headers/body to the browser. The extension additionally reaches two actual editor await boundaries. For later chunk reading, a browser fetch wrapper fetches the real owned artifact, clones its actual bytes, preserves real response headers/status, and schedules those same bytes through a two-pull ReadableStream with highWaterMark zero. It enqueues the first half and holds the second pull until settings ABA completes. With no prefetch buffering, the second pull is driven by the real editor reader.read. For SHA, the wrapper observes actual bytes from a clone without replacing the real Response; a crypto.subtle.digest wrapper matches the complete exact downloaded byte sequence, invokes the original native digest, and holds the caller-facing promise until settings ABA completes. Native crypto work can finish during the hold; this proves the editor digest-await result fence, not hardware computation still running. Other digest calls proceed immediately. Window fetch and crypto.subtle.digest are restored exactly in finally and the transient test property is deleted. Original route-held tests additionally cover destination ABA and actual email/workspace/actor changes; late-chunk/SHA extension specifically covers settings ABA. No product class or generation model was added; the final pageerror collector stays active without exception filtering. The injected scheduling code is static JavaScript to avoid tsx function serialization helpers.

# Whole-review boundaries: every line resolved with remaining cost


1. **Live HubSpot account footer values or ongoing agreement with them:**

Decision: Keep account_settings_verified=false; exact permitted account-settings validation remains required.

Cost: an actual permitted account-settings validation flow and evidence are still required.

2. **Account incorporation/legal status, eligible tier/trial, permissions and entitlement:**

Decision: Use the researched ordinary person/entity account conclusion only; paid-template tier/permissions and exact account rules remain unverified, with no purchase or account activation.

Cost: qualify an eligible account and exact current requirements without assuming free or production entitlement.

3. **Complete HubSpot template/HubL/filter acceptance, preview module requirements, unsubscribe behavior in a provider-rendered message, and native editing fidelity:**

Decision: Keep native_conformance_verified=false; require actual whole-template/HubL/provider conformance evidence before any required-template change or acceptance.

Cost: exact eligible-account native conformance, with documented provider behavior and any required template changes.

4. **Real inbox/client rendering and native plaintext handoff:**

Decision: Treat browser/plaintext bytes as local companions only; require real received-message/client and supported native plaintext proof.

Cost: received-message/client tests and supported native plaintext integration; no fidelity flag may be inferred from local SHA equality.

5. **OAuth/auth-mode approval, encrypted grants/credential resolution, provider API revision/schema and remote request lifecycle:**

Decision: Defer live auth/grants/provider schema lifecycle until secure approved contracts exist; do not invent an API or call providers.

Cost: separately authorized provider contracts and secure lifecycle implementation.

6. **Durable remote export/jobs, provider readback/content reconciliation, sender readiness and actual remote success:**

Decision: Keep remote_export_enabled=false; durable remote jobs/reconciliation/sender readiness remain unfinished, including independent implementation work.

Cost: complete adapter/worker/remote-account qualification and durable recovery; local audits are not remote receipts.

7. **Management URL/link conformance and production source publication:**

Decision: Keep management_link_verified=false/no URL; source publication remains separately unauthorized.

Cost: verified native links and separately authorized publication work.

8. **Other/international address layouts, blank-state addresses and inferred/normalized address components:**

Decision: Keep exact versioned comma/state/optional-field convention visible; future international formats need a supported new contract.

Cost: a new supported comparison/mapping contract rather than silently altering existing source or claiming provider-format equivalence.

9. **Raw/custom HTML, unresolved authored personalization and private/registered asset publication:**

Decision: Retain frozen refusal for unsupported raw/custom/tokens/private media; preserve source while separate safe mappings/public immutable asset delivery remain required.

Cost: dedicated safe destination mappings and qualified immutable public asset delivery; preserving original source is not acceptance of those exports.

10. **Conditional-comment/VML/style-block preservation demonstrated through a valid frozen fixture:**

# Whole-review boundaries: every line resolved with remaining cost


1. **Live HubSpot account footer values or ongoing agreement with them:**

Decision: Keep account_settings_verified=false; exact permitted account-settings validation remains required.

Cost: an actual permitted account-settings validation flow and evidence are still required.

2. **Account incorporation/legal status, eligible tier/trial, permissions and entitlement:**

Decision: Use the researched ordinary person/entity account conclusion only; paid-template tier/permissions and exact account rules remain unverified, with no purchase or account activation.

Cost: qualify an eligible account and exact current requirements without assuming free or production entitlement.

3. **Complete HubSpot template/HubL/filter acceptance, preview module requirements, unsubscribe behavior in a provider-rendered message, and native editing fidelity:**

Decision: Keep native_conformance_verified=false; require actual whole-template/HubL/provider conformance evidence before any required-template change or acceptance.

Cost: exact eligible-account native conformance, with documented provider behavior and any required template changes.

4. **Real inbox/client rendering and native plaintext handoff:**

Decision: Treat browser/plaintext bytes as local companions only; require real received-message/client and supported native plaintext proof.

Cost: received-message/client tests and supported native plaintext integration; no fidelity flag may be inferred from local SHA equality.

5. **OAuth/auth-mode approval, encrypted grants/credential resolution, provider API revision/schema and remote request lifecycle:**

Decision: Defer live auth/grants/provider schema lifecycle until secure approved contracts exist; do not invent an API or call providers.

Cost: separately authorized provider contracts and secure lifecycle implementation.

6. **Durable remote export/jobs, provider readback/content reconciliation, sender readiness and actual remote success:**

Decision: Keep remote_export_enabled=false; durable remote jobs/reconciliation/sender readiness remain unfinished, including independent implementation work.

Cost: complete adapter/worker/remote-account qualification and durable recovery; local audits are not remote receipts.

7. **Management URL/link conformance and production source publication:**

Decision: Keep management_link_verified=false/no URL; source publication remains separately unauthorized.

Cost: verified native links and separately authorized publication work.

8. **Other/international address layouts, blank-state addresses and inferred/normalized address components:**

Decision: Keep exact versioned comma/state/optional-field convention visible; future international formats need a supported new contract.

Cost: a new supported comparison/mapping contract rather than silently altering existing source or claiming provider-format equivalence.

9. **Raw/custom HTML, unresolved authored personalization and private/registered asset publication:**

Decision: Retain frozen refusal for unsupported raw/custom/tokens/private media; preserve source while separate safe mappings/public immutable asset delivery remain required.

Cost: dedicated safe destination mappings and qualified immutable public asset delivery; preserving original source is not acceptance of those exports.

10. **Conditional-comment/VML/style-block preservation demonstrated through a valid frozen fixture:**

Decision: Do not claim accepted VML/conditional/style-block fixtures; add valid supported fixtures if that source capability becomes available.

Cost: a valid supported source fixture if that source capability is introduced.

11. **Exact mapped-plaintext 2 MiB boundary and direct execution of every mapper defense after frozen validation:**

Decision: Retain explicit guards and state unreachable-branch coverage limits; no fabricated plaintext-boundary/defense proof.

Cost: valid future reachable fixtures; the explicit guard and source refusal tests are present, but these branches are not claimed directly covered.

12. **Restoration of Task 1's complete initial broad-suite/RED/intermediate/GREEN output or undoing its prohibited broad attempt:**

Decision: Preserve permanent Task1 scope-violation/lost-history disclosure; current848-test proof cannot reconstruct prior history.

Cost: permanently reduced historical auditability; retain the disclosure and separate current checks/original-preservation evidence. No rerun can reconstruct that history.

13. **Complete before-implementation behavioral RED for Task 4 helpers:**

Decision: Preserve permanent Task4 historical behavioral-RED qualification; current real tests remain independently useful.

Cost: permanent historical process qualification; current real tests and mounted evidence remain valid.

14. **Reconstructing original run-4 pageerror stacks and treating earlier failed/partial Task 5 runs as current acceptance:**

Decision: Retain historical run4 messages/available files only; current complete collection does not recreate old stacks or upgrade failed runs.

Cost: retain the exact limited evidence; current unfiltered stack collection and current passing run do not recreate old stacks.

15. **Comprehensive inherited auth/RLS/fixture/SDK auditing beyond the changed integrations:**

Decision: Attribute existing focused auth/RLS/SDK checks accurately; broader security/concurrency qualification stays open.

Cost: broader security/concurrency qualification remains separate when required.

16. **Rollback of an already committed audit after client-side response cancellation:**

Decision: Claim client refusal only; no rollback of already committed audit and no stronger transactional guarantee.

Cost: any stronger transactional cancellation requirement needs a separate contract and evidence; no such rollback is claimed.

17. **Browser WebSocket, server-process and OS-wide egress isolation:**

Decision: Claim routed browser HTTP/SW guard only; comprehensive process/socket/WS egress isolation remains unqualified.

Cost: process/network isolation qualification before claiming comprehensive no-egress behavior. No provider request was needed or run for this review.

18. **Production body-transfer load, aggregate memory/concurrency, authority-lock occupancy, broader accessibility and cross-browser performance budgets:**

Decision: Retain bounded30second lock/read and narrow mobile/Viewer evidence; production load/memory/accessibility/performance acceptance stays open.

Cost: production operational/accessibility acceptance. Mobile390 and Viewer checks are narrower local evidence.

19. **Production bundle-size acceptance and inherited dependency maintenance/security status:**

Decision: Keep unminified size and inherited Docker dependency deprecations visible; no global warning-free/budget/security/upgrade acceptance.

Cost: release budgets and dependency assessment remain separate; no dependency upgrade/security conclusion is inferred.

20. **Fresh Linux runtime HTTP/PG/Chromium behavior under network-none, additional architectures, and independent rederivation of OCI configs:**

Decision: Retain Linuxarm64 source/packaging/network-none scope and root verified distinct config/index attribution; no runtime HTTP/PG/extra platform claim.

Cost: additional runtime/platform evidence when required; no duplicate answered image checks were run here.

21. **Independent database/private-file preservation audit and exhaustive rehash of every historic report/artifact:**

Decision: Attribute original-data/private-METADATA and evidence hash verification to root; no new private-content/reviewer audit claim.

Cost: attribution remains to root's completed verification and preserved manifests, not a new reviewer execution claim.

22. **Canonical worktree/Desktop fast-forward, fresh canonical qualification, owned-app restoration, final evidence archival and cleanup:**

Decision: Finish guarded canonical delivery/restoration/retention and qualify actual destination before closing Task6.

Cost: finish the planned guarded delivery sequence and record actual results; this review does not assert that delivery already occurred.

23. **Public push/deployment, historical publication approval rejection and remote CI coverage of this local head:**

Decision: Do not push/deploy/retry rejected publication or claim old remote CI covers local head; exact-head release evidence remains separate.

Cost: separate authorization and exact-head release evidence, without retrying rejected publication implicitly.

24. **Completion of Baseline A's 65 requirements, all 13 production gates or unrelated remaining GA work:**

Decision: Preserve full65/all13/zero accepted and continue remaining independent PRD work; no local slice is full production acceptance.

Cost: continue the full program; zero whole requirements/gates become accepted here, and production startup must remain closed.
